# JS/TS legacy systematic-scope closure accounting — 2026-09-08

## Result and boundary

Exact stratum-membership join of the eight requested groups yields **1,751 unique original IDs: 1,024 currently held and 727 surviving**, at **2,721 corpus holds** (2,718 additive plus historical Go generation-0165 and Rust generation-0161/0224). Groups overlap; group totals are not additive. The historical three come from `datasets/cache/expansion-v1/review-partition-v1/manifest.json`, not from the older sample ledger alone.

All 727 survivors join existing per-ID review evidence. This accounting isolates **43 IDs / 44 scoped questions requiring targeted contract reads or a more explicit criterion disposition**; they are listed below. The other **684 IDs retain existing scoped findings without a newly identified question in these eight groups**. That latter number is NOT 684 fresh reviews or a universal admission pass. In particular, absence of a documented question is not proof of all seven criteria. The eight property-copy evidence gaps below explicitly distinguish insufficient closure evidence from a demonstrated defect.

Read-only Gate 2 work apart from this isolated report. No candidate executions, fresh behavioral metrics, central edits, new holds, cloud actions, commits or unique-row credit. Main owns final integration and any final closure seal. Only this report is intended to change. Scope inventory is historical membership, not a live backlog.

## Criterion-specific interpretation

- S: explicit for-of/indexed validation, conversion to dense storage, JSON/split-derived inputs, private dense arrays, and expressly ignoring malformed entries support the recorded negative findings. Typed inputs with no promised malformed-slot policy do not automatically require a rejection test. A genuine unresolved callback/slot contract is separated below.
- I: registration records and per-handle unsubscribe semantics matter, not simply function identity. Explicit first-occurrence `off(name, listener)` is a different API: JavaScript repair-0235 has a documented passing control and remains supported. Reentrant callbacks are not automatically required to be shielded. Three duplicate-handle questions remain below; later elementary positives do not resolve them.
- A/J: original specification remains authoritative. A passing reference or matching test cannot decide missing interface signatures, test-selected fields, added overloads or incompatible synchronous/asynchronous validation. Explicit supplied-source repair behavior is relevant, but contradictory generation APIs are not silently inherited from tests.
- N: exact integer arithmetic, explicitly prescribed native formulas, checked/saturated arithmetic and input-only finiteness conditions do not imply an extra finite-output guarantee. Unreachable counter lifetime, necessarily huge outputs and arbitrary callback mutation are not finite-intermediate bugs. No new N contract-read requirement was isolated in this surviving scope from the existing per-ID findings; prior nonnumeric questions are preserved.
- P/C: null-prototype maps, own-key checks, data descriptors, field-by-field construction, explicit enumerable/shallow scope and retained opaque identity support scoped findings. Generic 'copies records' is not sufficient to certify preservation of required non-enumerable/inherited fields: eight such evidence gaps are explicitly queued below. Required-key handling for TypeScript generation-0078 remains distinct from its deliberately enumerable-only unknown-extra policy.
- U: the surviving notes identify no unresolved contradictory suffix allocator. Exact membership alone does not mean every member generates names. Existing uniqueness/wrapping/alias findings are retained, not replaced by a substring heuristic. TypeScript elementary notes are scoped nontriviality evidence; their 'suffix' diagnostics concern terminal regex characters, not automatically suffix-allocation uniqueness.

## Retained caveats outside the assigned membership question

Do not turn this report into global clearance of separately documented issues. Examples: JavaScript generation-0112 shallow/RESET alias wording, generation-0113 hidden selected-key interpretation, generation-0270 extra part-field copying, generation-0307 exception-class conflict, and repair-0207 stale duplicate unsubscribe handles have legacy caveats, but their exact memberships here do not include the corresponding copy/API/subscription group. Those remain available for main's broader final issue ledger rather than being silently solved or counted as this group's scoped question. Likewise unrelated Unicode, malformed URL, arbitrary custom accessor and deep-recursion caveats remain in the cited notes. This is not permission to suppress a known concrete defect merely because it falls outside a historical group.
